1 The review excludes the extra-high dose (EH, Group 5) group from the evaluation and discussions of the 
carcinogenicity results of BCX7353. This is to enhance the clarity of the discussion and evaluation of the 
study results. The EH group primarily provides information that supports the dose selection of the 
carcinogenicity study design. This group was terminated for humane reasons prematurely in Week 28 
(Day 191). By the time of termination of the animals, the group mean body weights was decreased by 
16.2% and 8.7% in males and females, respectively. Five of the females died. The dosing regimen was 
60 and 40 mg/kg/day for weeks 1 – 17 and 18 – 27, respectively. No data relevant to tumorigenicity was 
obtained from any of the animals.  
Animals in this EH group generally showed clinical observations similar to that of the HD group. These 
included decreases in body weight and food consumption, salivation, thin appearance and audible 
breathing. Microscopic changes, however, were unremarkable because the findings were generally less 
severe and with lower incidence.

OBSERVATIONS AND RESULTS 
Mortality
The HD group in males and females showed higher incidence of unscheduled deaths 
(Table 4). The respective number of deaths in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups was 17, 
21, 18, and 26 in males, and 25, 24, 23, and 32 in the females. The respective survival 
rate in the HD group and remaining groups was 55% and 65 – 70% in males, and 47% 
and 58 – 62% in females. In short, the HD group showed mortality rate at least 10 – 12% 
higher than other groups. None of the deaths were considered HS-related.

Clinical Signs 
The HD group showed increases in incidence of audible breathing. The respective 
incidence of audible breathing in the C, LO, MD, and HD groups (observations/animals) 
was 5/4, 1/1, 1/1, and 1160/47 in males and 3/3, 3/3, 2/2 and 470/45 in females .

The HD group showed decreases in body weights (Table 5 and Fig 2 ). The HD males 
and females showed decreases of 17.6% and 13.5% in mean body w eight (vs. control) 
at the end of the study .

Histopathology 
Peer Review 
Yes. Keith Nelson, DVM, PhD, conducted a peer review of the pathology report. 
Neoplastic Findings
Table 6 presents notable neoplastic findings in the 2-year study. Appendices B and C 
(provided by Dr. Zhuang Miao, the Statistical Reviewer) list the overall tumor incidence in 
each sex in the study. Dose-related increases in the incidence of the following tumors 
were observed: HS in multiple organs (mostly in the mesenteric lymph nodes) in both 
males and females, undifferentiated carcinoma in the skin in males, and endometrial 
stromal carcinoma in the females.

Hemangiosarcomas (HS): Table 7 presents the distribution of HS in the study. The 
mesenteric lymph node is the primary location of HS. It accounted for 89% (16/18) of the 
tumors in the HD group. Five (5/18) rats with HS either died or were sacrificed 
prematurely; none of the deaths, however, were attributed to HS by the pathologist.

Skin tumors: Three HD males (#4013, #4033, and #4053) showed undifferentiated 
carcinoma in the skin. These rats were sacrificed on Days 730 (terminal), 489 (moribund), 
and 601 (moribund). The report identified these tumor as “mortality-independent” primary 
tumors. None of these rats had HS. The statistical analysis showed p values of 0.045 
for trend test and 0.107 for pairwise comparison between the HD and control. This tumor 
type did not meet any criterion for a positive result. 
Uterine tumors: Two MD and three HD females showed endometrial stromal sarcoma. 
The tumor was the cause of death in 4 of the 5 rats (2 in both MD and HD). The 2 MD 
rats (#3522 and #3548) were sacrificed on Days 601 and 606, respectively. The 2 HD 
rats (#4511 and #4520) were sacrificed on days 722 and 713, respectively. The remaining 
HD rat (#4528) was sacrificed as scheduled. This tumor type did not meet any criterion 
for a positive result. 
Non-Neoplastic
Table 8 summarizes notable non-neoplastic findings. Treatment-related lesions were 
observed in the following organs and tissues: adrenal glands, kidney, lung, lymph nodes, 
skeletal muscle, intestine, and spleen. The most common finding is vacuolated or foamy 
macrophages (adrenals, lymph nodes, intestine, and spleen) and is observed mostly in 
the HD group. Other findings included accumulation of alveolar macrophages and 
exudate in the nasal cavity in both sexes and myofiber degeneration of the skeletal 
muscles in males.

Toxicokinetics: Table 9 presents berotralstat exposure data in rats in the first year. Both 
mean AUC and Cmax were dose-proportional in week 1 but supra proportional to dose in 
weeks 13 and 52. The exposures were the highest in week 52. Data indicate that 
berotralstat bioaccumulates in rats.

8.2 Transgenic Mouse Study (20088905) 
Study title: BCX7353: A 26-Week Oral Oncogenicity Study in Tg.rasH2 mice 
Study no.: 20088905 
Study report location: 4.2.3.4.1

Key Study Findings 
86F1 Tg.RasH2 mice dosed orally withs; 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 weeks did not 
show any evidence of carcinogenicity. Mice were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle: 
deionized water), 8, 20, and 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 weeks. There were no 
significant dose-related increases in the incidence of any tumors.

NDA #214094 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
Adequacy of Carcinogenicity Study
This study was adequate. The study design was routine for transgenic models. The 
treatment duration was 26 weeks. There were 25 mice/sex/dose. The Agency concurred 
with the dose selection. See nonclinical review completed by Dr. Dong Zhao on 
December 14, 2017 (DARRTS ID 4195500). 
Appropriateness of Test Models
The model is appropriate. The Executive CAC reviewed the model and dose selection 
in the December 14, 2017 meeting. See nonclinical review completed by Dr. Dong Zhao 
on December 14, 2017 (DARRTS ID 4195500).  
Evaluation of Tumor Findings
B6F1 Tg.RasH2 mice dosed by oral gavage with up to 50-mg/kg/day berotralstat for 26 
weeks showed no dose-related increases in the incidence of any tumors. A positive 
control (75 mg/kg/day N-nitroso-N-methylurea, or NMU) group showed increases in the 
incidence of tumors typical of the treatment in this model. Berotralstat was considered 
negative for carcinogenicity in the Tg.RasH2 mouse model. 
The HD male group treated with berotralstat showed a higher incidence of HS in the 
spleen (2/25), but the C and HD females showed similar incidences (3/25 and 2/25, 
respectively).  The finding is not considered significant or treatment-related. 
Many tissues in 6 HD males were not examined because tissues were missing. The 
missing tissues included pituitary, thymus, thyroid/parathyroid, adrenals, lungs, trachea, 
gallbladder, urinary bladder, rectum, mesenteric lymph nodes, prostate, epididymides, 
and larynx. See Protocol deviation. The lack of tumor information in these organs would 
not probably affect the validity of the study results and the overall interpretation of study 
findings. 
Methods 
Doses: 0, 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day, see Table 10. 
Frequency of dosing: Daily 
Dose volume: 2.5 mL/kg 
Route of administration: Oral gavage 
Formulation/Vehicle: Aqueous solution/ deionized water 
The NMU concentration in the PC group was 7.5-mg/mL in 
citrate-buffered saline (ph4.5).

OBSERVATION AND RESULTS 
Mortality
Not remarkable. There were no significant differences in mortality between the vehicle 
control and the berotralstat treatment groups. The number of mice that survived to the 
terminal sacrifice was 22 - 25 out of 25 in the control and berotralstat treatment groups, 
respectively, Table 11. Four animals in the berotralstat treatment groups (1-HD M, 2 MD 
Fs, and 1 HD F) died of gavage accidents. Berotralstat treatment groups showed no 
apparent increases in tumor-related deaths.

Neoplastic Findings
There were no significant, dose-related increases in any tumors in the berotralstat 
treatment groups (Tables 13 and 14). The PC group showed marked increases in tumor 
incidences in both males and females. The tumors in the PC groups included squamous 
cell papilloma and malignant lymphomas.

Non-Neoplastic
Non-neoplastic findings were noticed in the nasal cavity, eye, liver, lymph nodes, and 
spleen (Table 15). The findings in the nose included inflammation in all berotralstat  
treatment groups in both sexes and erosion/ulceration of respiratory epithelium in the HD 
males. Findings in the eyes (dysplasia), liver (inflammation and hepatocellular atrophy), 
lymph nodes (decreases in cellularity) and spleen (decreases in cellularity) were minimal 
in severity, low in incidence or occurred in only one sex. They also lacked significant 
dose-response relationship.

Dosing Solution Analysis: Dosing solutions were analyzed in weeks 1, 4, 13, and 26. 
Berotralstat concentrations ranged from 83.9% to 110% of the target concentration. 
Toxicokinetics 
Toxicokinetics of berotralstat was studied in satellite groups in non-transgenic mice. Blood 
samples were collected at hours 0, 1, 2, 4, 8, and 24 post dosing on days 1 and 182 (n = 
3 mice/time point). Table 16 presents AUC and Cmax data. Both mean AUC and Cmax 
were dose-proportional in week 1 but supra proportional to dose in week 26. It is unknown 
whether the data reflect the plasma drug levels in the transgenic mice.

11 Integrated Summary and Safety Evaluation 
The carcinogenic potential of berotralstat was evaluated in a traditional 2-yr bioassay in 
rats and a 26-week alternative assay in a transgenic mouse model. The respective 
berotralstat dose in the LD, MD, and HD groups was 3, 8, and 20 mg/kg/day in rats and 
8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day in mice. There were 60 and 25 animals/sex/dose in rats and 
Tg.rasH2 transgenic mice, respectively. The results showed that berotralstat was 
carcinogenic in male rats, but not in female rats, nor in transgenic mouse. Relevance of 
the tumorigenicity data in rats to humans is unknown. 
22
 
Reference ID: 4580256 

[PDF PAGE 246]
   
   
   
       
  
   
  
     
 
  
 
      
   
  
 
 
   
 
 
 
   
  
  
 
  
  
   
 
  
    
  
 
     
NDA #214094 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
The carcinogenicity of berotralstat in male rats was demonstrated by dose-related 
increases in the incidence of HS in the mesenteric lymph node (incidence: 1, 1, 2, and 9 
in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups, respectively). Only the increase in the HS incidence 
in the HD group reached statistical significance (p < 0.01 in pairwise comparison). The 
increase in the HS incidence in the whole body in the HD group reached statistically 
marginal significance in pairwise comparison (p = 0.017). Both were positive in the trend 
analysis (p < 0.005). 
The Applicant considers berotralstat an epigenetic carcinogen in rats. The review agrees 
with the Applicant. The review, however, disagrees with the Applicant about the relevance 
of the tumorigenic findings to humans. The Exec. CAC agreed with the review. See 
minutes of the Exec. CAC meeting dated March 10, 2020 (DARRTS ID# 4576054). 
The applicant contends that the HS finding is irrelevant to humans and of no safety 
concern for the proposed clinical use of the drug. Section 13.1 of the proposed labeling 
states “[t]his finding is considered rodent specific”. The Applicant argues that their 
conclusion was based on the mechanism of tumorigenesis of HS in rodents. Specifically, 
HS in rats is secondary to hypoxia in rats, which will not happen in humans receiving 
berotralstat treatment. There is evidence for and against this argument.  
Primary evidence in support of the hypoxia hypothesis is a paper by Cohen et al. 
published in 2009 [Toxicol Sci, 2009;111(1)4-18]. This is a review article (referred to as 
the Cohen paper hereafter) discussing the mechanism of HS formation associated with 
non-genotoxic compounds in rodents and the relevance of the HS in rodents to humans. 
Both genotoxic and non-genotoxic compounds can induce HS in rodents (mostly in mice). 
The mechanism of carcinogenesis of HS differs between genotoxic and non-genotoxic 
compounds. Non-genotoxic compounds can produce HS in mice secondary to tissue 
hypoxia. Such compounds include pesticides, industrial chemicals, and pharmaceuticals. 
Pharmaceuticals include “calcium channel blockers, antipsychotics, PDE-5 inhibitors, 
DPP-4 inhibitors, antiarrhythmics, gonadotropin receptor antagonists, antisense 
compounds, nitric oxide releasers, hemolytic compounds, and vascular endothelial 
growth factor (VEGF) inducers.”  
The Cohen paper concludes that hypoxia and macrophage activation is a common 
mechanism of action of non-genotoxic drug-induced HS in rodents. The paper states the 
carcinogenesis appears to “be a convergence of multiple initiating events (e.g., 
hemolysis, decreased respiration, adipocyte growth) leading to either dysregulated 
angiogenesis and/or erythropoiesis that results from hypoxia and macrophage activation. 
These events lead to the release of angiogenic growth factors and cytokines that 
stimulate endothelial cell proliferation, which, if sustained, provide the milieu that can lead 
to HS formation.” 
The Cohen paper discussed in depth the hypoxia-related HS induced by two 
pharmaceuticals: pregabalin and troglitazone. Pregabalin (Lyrica, NDA 21-446) caused 
HS in the tissues where spontaneous HS are commonly observed in mice: liver, spleen, 
23
 
Reference ID: 4580256 

[PDF PAGE 247]
 
  
   
  
  
   
  
   
 
   
    
  
  
     
     
  
   
 
  
   
    
  
 
 
 
   
     
 
 
 
   
  
   
  
   
  
  
NDA #214094 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
and bone marrow in B6C3F1 and CD1 mice, but not any tissue in rats.2 The paper states: 
“The precipitating event in pregabalin-associated HS development in mice appears to be 
decreased respiratory function, including decreased respiration rate and decreased 
minute volumes, along with alterations in acid-base balance, gradually shifting to a 
relative alkalotic state.” This eventually leads to local tissue hypoxia.  
Troglitazone (Rezulin, withdrawn from the market) produced primarily HS in the skin 
(subcutaneous adipose tissue) in mice, but not rats. Troglitazone produced local tissue 
hypoxia in mice “due to the inability of angiogenesis to keep pace with growth of the fat 
pad. The local tissue hypoxia results in [hypoxia-inducible factor] HIF-1a activation which 
can activate VEGF secretion and/or macrophage activation”. 
The Applicant argues that HS in rats treated with berotralstat was also a result of hypoxia, 
which occurred because of narrowing of the nasal airway passage and breathing difficulty. 
Narrowing of nasal airway passage was demonstrated by the presence of exudate in the 
nasal cavity in berotralstat -treated rats in microscopic examinations. The respective 
incidence of the presence of luminal exudate in nasal cavity in the C, LD, MD, and HD 
groups (out of 60) was 2, 2, 8, and 23 in males and 1, 1, 4, 25 in females. Breathing 
difficulty was demonstrated by audible breathing. The respective incidence of audible 
breathing in the C, LD, MD, and HD groups (observations/animals) was 5/4, 1/1, 1/1, and 
1160/47 in males and 3/3, 3/3, 2/2, and 470/45 in females. 
The review agrees with the Applicant that the HD rats showed breathing difficulty, which 
suggest the presence of hypoxia. Rodents are obligated nose-breathing animals. 
Presence of exudate in the nasal passage may impede the airflow and causing breathing 
difficulty which is indicated by the observation of audible breathing. The difficulty of 
breathing may not indicate necessarily tissue hypoxia in rats. 
There are also factors arguing against applying the hypoxia hypothesis to the 
interpretation of the berotralstat study results in rats. This includes differences in animal 
species, tumor locations, and drug classes. Table 17 presents pharmaceuticals that 
induce HS in the Cohen paper. None of these drugs induced HS in rats. Regarding to 
tumor locations, the Cohen paper reported that HS “most frequently occur in liver, spleen, 
heart, or subcutaneous adipose tissue”, which is consistent with the findings observed 
with these drugs. Further, mice have higher spontaneous incidence of HS than rats or 
humans because they have lower antioxidant levels (e.g., Vitamin E) than rats and 
humans. The high spontaneous background rate may contribute to their enhanced 
susceptibility to developing HS. 
However, berotralstat does not belong to any of the HS-inducing drug classes. The rat 
has low spontaneous HS rates. Further, rats and mice differ in organs/tissues with HS. 
Hypoxia could have been present in rats treated with high dose of berotralstat, but the 
2 Section 5 of the Label of pregabalin states: “In standard preclinical in vivo lifetime carcinogenicity studies 
of LYRICA, an unexpectedly high incidence of hemangiosarcoma was identified in two different strains 
of mice [see Nonclinical Toxicology (13.1)]. The clinical significance of this finding is unknown.” 
24
 
Reference ID: 4580256 

[PDF PAGE 248]
 
   
   
 
 
 
 
 
 
  
   
   
 
   
 
  
  
 
 
 
  
    
   
     
 
 
NDA #214094 	 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
hypoxia hypothesis alone cannot fully explain the mechanism of HS formation in rats. The 
lymph node specificity of HS in rats could be related to the high local concentration of the 
parent compound and metabolites, alone or in combination.

There is insufficient evidence to support the Applicant’s argument that HS in the 
mesenteric lymph node associated with berotralstat treatment in male rats is a species-
specific finding. Thus, relevance of the carcinogenic finding in rats to humans is unknown 
at present time.

Executive CAC Protocol Minutes 
Date of Meeting: December 12, 2017 
Committee: Karen Davis Bruno, PhD, OND IO, Chair

The following information reflects a brief summary of the Committee discussion and its 
recommendations. 
The Committee did not address the sponsor’s proposed statistical evaluation for the 2-yr 
carcinogenicity bioassays, as this does not affect the sponsor’s ability to initiate the 
bioassays. The sponsor may seek guidance on the statistical evaluation of bioassay 
results from agency staff separately. Data files should be submitted electronically 
following the CDER/CBER Guidance for Industry, Providing Regulatory Submissions in 
Electronic Format - Standardized Study Data (December 2014) and the latest Study Data 
Technical Conformance Guide. 
IND# 135058 
Drug Name: BCX7353
Sponsor: Biocryst Pharmaceuticals, Inc. 
Background
Biocryst Pharmaceuticals is developing BCX7353, a small molecule kallikrein inhibitor, as 
an oral treatment of hereditary angioedema (HAE) under IND 135058. Kallikrein is a 
plasma enzyme that regulates the production of bradykinin. Bradykinin, as a vasodilatory 
agent, binds to the bradykinin B2 receptor and results in dilation of blood vessel, fluid 
leakage, and edema. BCX7353 was negative in the in vitro bacterial mutagenicity (Ames 
test), in vitro mammalian cell mutagenicity (human peripheral blood lymphocytes mutation 
assay) studies, and in vivo rat bone marrow micronucleus study. In the present 
submission, the sponsor submitted a Special Protocol Assessment request (SD 13 
received 11/14/2017) for a carcinogenicity study evaluating BCX7353 in RasH2 
transgenic (Tg.RasH2) mice. 
Tg.rasH2 Mouse Carcinogenicity Study Dose Selection
BCX7353 dose selection is supported by the results of a 4-week toxicity study in 
Tg.RasH2 wildtype littermate mice (Study 20088903) that evaluated oral gavage doses 
of 0 (vehicle of water), 25, 50 or 125 mg/kg/day. The maximal tolerable dose (MTD) has 
been identified as 50 mg/kg/day based on mortalities, clinical signs and body weight 
decreases at 125 mg/kg/day and slight decreases in body weight gain at 50 mg/kg/day. 
26
 
Reference ID: 4580256 

[PDF PAGE 250]
 
 
  
 
   
   
   
 
   
 
                                              
NDA #214094 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
Biocryst is proposing dose levels of 0, 8, 20 and 60 mg/kg/day for the 6-month 
carcinogenicity study in Tg.RasH2 hemizygous mice. The sponsor’s proposed high-dose 
was set at one-half the lethal dose of 125 mg/kg/day. 
Executive CAC Recommendations and Conclusions 
Tg.rasH2 Mouse 
 The Committee recommended doses of 0, 8, 20, and 50 mg/kg/day for males and 
females with the high dose based on providing approximately one-third the 
exposure of the lethal dose of 125 mg/kg in the 28-day mouse toxicology study. 
 The mid- and low-doses for both sexes were selected based on adequate spacing 
of AUC. 
 The Committee noted that hematology at the end of the study is not needed for 
FDA. 
 The Committee noted that the use of toxicokinetic dose groups is not needed for 
the FDA. 
 The sponsor should conduct histopathological examination in all dose groups.  
Karen Davis Bruno, PhD 
Chair, Executive CAC

Appendix D. Minutes of the March 10, 2020, Exec CAC Meeting dated. 
Executive CAC Final Study Minutes
Date of Meeting: March 10, 2020 
Committee:	 Karen Davis Bruno, PhD, OND IO, Chair 
Paul Brown, PhD, OND IO, Member 
Tim McGovern, PhD, OND IO, Member 
Ron Wange, PhD, OND IO, Member 
Federica Basso, PhD, DMEP, Alternate Member 
Carol Galvis, PhD, DPARP, Pharm/Tox Team Leader 
Luqi Pei, PhD, DPARP, Presenting Reviewer 
The following information reflects a brief summary of the Committee discussion and its 
recommendations. 
Application Type and Number(s):  NDA 214094 
Drug Name: Berotralstat (BCX7353) 
Sponsor: BioCryst Pharmaceuticals 
Background
BioCryst Pharmaceuticals filed NDA 214094 on December 3, 2019 seeking marketing 
approval of berotralstat, a kallikrein inhibitor, for the prevention of hereditary angioedema 
in adult and adolescent patients. The carcinogenicity potential of berotralstat was 
evaluated in a 2-year traditional bioassay in rats and in a 26-week study in transgenic 
mice (Reports #806-161 and #20088905, respectively). The Agency concurred with the 
dose selection of the protocol of the 26-week study in transgenic mice on December 14, 
2017. 
Mouse Carcinogenicity Study
B6F1 Tg.rasH2 mice (25/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage 0 (vehicle: deionized 
water), 8, 20, and 50-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 26 weeks. No evidence of 
tumorigenicity was found in either sex. A positive control (75 mg/kg/day N-nitroso-N­
methylurea) group showed increases in the incidence of tumors typical of the treatment 
in this model. 
Rat Carcinogenicity Study  
Rats (60/sex/dose) were dosed by oral gavage with 0 (vehicle: deionized water), 3, 8, and 
20-mg/kg berotralstat once daily for 104 weeks. The male rats showed statistically 
significant, dose-related increases in the incidence of hemangiosarcomas (HS) in the 
mesenteric lymph nodes (Table 1). In pairwise comparisons between the high dose (HD) 
and controls (C), only the increase in the HS incidence in the mesenteric lymph nodes 
reached statistical significance (p < 0.01); the test for HS in the whole body reached a 
marginal significance (p=0.017); the test for the HS and hemangioma in combination in 
the whole body did not reach statistical significance (p=0.041). The trend analyses for all 
the above tumor findings were significant (p < 0.005). 
34
 
Reference ID: 4580256 

[PDF PAGE 258]
   
 
  
  
     
  
   
  
   
   
 
 
       
   
   
    
   
 
  
 
   
 
  
     
    
 
 
NDA #214094 	 Reviewer: Luqi Pei, PhD 
The high dose rats in the study showed clinical signs of labored and audible breathing 
during the study. Microscopic examination revealed presence of nasal exudate and sinus 
histiocytosis in the mesenteric lymph node. These findings suggest potential presence of 
tissue hypoxia in these rats. 
The relevance of the HS findings associated with berotralstat treatment in rats to human 
is unknown. Cohen et al [Toxicol Sci , 2009;111(1)4-18] reported that HS and 
hemangiomas associated with non-genotoxic chemicals in rodents may be attributed to 
hypoxia. Hypoxia that appeared present in the high dose in rats may play a significant 
role in the carcinogenesis of berotralstat, but tissue specificity of the finding suggests that 
other factors may also play important roles. These factors may include higher exposures 
of the tissue to the parent compound, metabolites, and/or drug impurities. Overall, 
relevance of the HS finding to humans remains unknown.

Executive CAC Conclusions 
Mouse study: 
 The Committee concluded the carcinogenicity study is adequate and negative for 
drug-related neoplasms in males and females. 
Rat study: 
	 The Committee concluded the carcinogenicity study is adequate. 
	 The Committee concluded that berotralstat is positive for drug-related neoplasms 
in male rats. The high dose of berotralstat (20 mg/kg/day) caused a statistically 
significant increase in hemangiosarcoma in male rats in the mesenteric lymph 
node. ExecCAC criteria for statistical significance were not reached for 
hemangiosarcoma in all tissues or for hemangiosarcoma and hemangioma 
combined in all tissues. 
	 The Committee concluded that the mechanism of carcinogenesis of berotralstat in 
rats remains unclear and that the relevance of berotralstat-induced tumorigenesis 
in rats to humans is unknown.  
	 The Committee concluded that berotralstat tested negative for drug-related 
neoplasms in female rats.  
Karen Davis Bruno, PhD 
Chair, Executive CAC
